swapid - Corrige las cadenas de identificación ATA (nombre del dispositivo, número de serie, versión de firmware) intercambiadas de bytes devueltas por algunos controladores de dispositivo defectuosos.

-P TYPE, --presets=TYPE
[Solo ATA] Especifica si smartctl debe usar alguna opción preestablecida disponible para esta unidad. Por defecto, si la unidad se reconoce en la base de datos de smartmontools, se utilizan los ajustes preestablecidos.

El argumento show mostrará las opciones preestablecidas para tu unidad y el argumento showall mostrará todas las unidades conocidas en la base de datos de smartmontools, junto con sus opciones preestablecidas. Si no hay ajustes preestablecidos para tu unidad y crees que debería haberlos (por ejemplo, se necesita una opción -v o -F para que smartctl muestre los valores correctos) entonces por favor contacta a los desarrolladores de smartmontools para que esta información pueda ser añadida a la base de datos de smartmontools. La información de contacto está al final de esta página del manual.

Los argumentos válidos para esta opción son:

use - si se reconoce una unidad, se utilizan los ajustes preestablecidos almacenados para ella. Este es el valor por defecto. Ten en cuenta que los ajustes preestablecidos NO anularán las opciones de interpretación adicional de Atributos ('-v N,algo') de la línea de comandos ni las opciones explícitas '-F' de la línea de comandos.

ignore - no usar ajustes preestablecidos.

show - muestra si la unidad se reconoce en la base de datos y, en caso afirmativo, sus ajustes preestablecidos, y luego sale.

showall - lista todas las unidades reconocidas y los ajustes preestablecidos que están configurados para ellas, y luego sale. Esto también verifica las expresiones regulares y la configuración de la base de datos de unidades en busca de errores de sintaxis.

La opción '-P showall' acepta hasta dos argumentos opcionales para coincidir con un tipo de unidad y versión de firmware específicos. El comando:

  smartctl -P showall
lista todas las entradas, el comando: 
  smartctl -P showall 'MODEL'
lista todas las entradas que coinciden con MODEL, y el comando: 
  smartctl -P showall 'MODEL' 'FIRMWARE'
lista todas las entradas para este MODEL y una versión de FIRMWARE específica.
-B [+]FILE, --drivedb=[+]FILE
[Solo ATA] Lee la base de datos de unidades del ARCHIVO. La nueva base de datos reemplaza la base de datos integrada por defecto. Si se especifica '+', entonces las nuevas entradas preceden a las entradas integradas.

Las entradas opcionales se leen del archivo /etc/smart_drivedb.h si esta opción no se especifica.

Si /usr/share/smartmontools/drivedb.h está presente, se utiliza el contenido de este archivo en lugar de la tabla integrada.

Ejecuta /usr/bin/update-smart-drivedb para actualizar este archivo desde el repositorio SVN de smartmontools.

Los archivos de base de datos utilizan la misma sintaxis de C/C++ que se utiliza para inicializar la base de datos integrada. Se permiten comentarios estilo C/C++. Ejemplo:

  /* Entrada completa: */
  {
    "Model family",    // Información sobre la familia/serie del modelo.
    "MODEL1.*REGEX",   // Expresión regular para coincidir con el modelo del dispositivo.
    "VERSION.*REGEX",  // Expresión regular para coincidir con la(s) versión(es) de firmware.
    "Some warning",    // Mensaje de advertencia.
    "-v 9,minutes"     // Cadena de opciones preestablecidas -v y -F.
  },
  /* Entrada mínima: */
  {
    "",                // Sin información de familia/serie del modelo.
    "MODEL2.*REGEX",   // Expresión regular para coincidir con el modelo del dispositivo.
    "",                // Todas las versiones de firmware.
    "",                // Sin advertencia.
    ""                 // Sin opciones preestablecidas.
  },
  /* Entrada de ID USB: */
  {
    "USB: Device; Bridge", // Información sobre el dispositivo USB y el nombre del puente.
    "0x1234:0xabcd",   // Expresión regular para coincidir con el ID del proveedor:producto.
    "0x0101",          // Expresión regular para coincidir con bcdDevice.
    "",                // No utilizado.
    "-d sat"           // Cadena con la opción de tipo de dispositivo.
  },
  /* ... */
PRUEBA/ABORTAR PRUEBA SIN CONEXIÓN Y OPCIONES DE AUTOPRUEBA:
-t TEST, --test=TEST
Ejecuta TEST inmediatamente. La opción '-C' se puede usar en conjunto con esta opción para ejecutar las autocomprobaciones cortas o largas (y también para dispositivos ATA, selectivas o de transporte) en modo cautivo (conocido como "modo foreground" para dispositivos SCSI). Ten en cuenta que solo se puede ejecutar un tipo de prueba a la vez, por lo que solo se debe especificar un tipo de prueba por línea de comandos. Ten en cuenta también que si un ordenador se apaga o se reinicia durante una autocomprobación, no debería haber ningún daño. La autocomprobación se abortará o se reanudará automáticamente.

Todos los comandos '-t TEST' se pueden dar durante la operación normal del sistema a menos que se use el modo cautivo (opción '-C'). Sin embargo, una autocomprobación en curso puede degradar el rendimiento de la unidad. Las solicitudes de E/S frecuentes del sistema operativo aumentan la duración de una prueba. Estos impactos pueden variar de un dispositivo a otro.

Si ocurre un fallo en la prueba, el dispositivo puede suspender las pruebas e informar el resultado inmediatamente.

[ATA] Ten en cuenta que el comando ATA SMART EXECUTE OFF-LINE IMMEDIATE (el comando para iniciar una prueba) fue declarado obsoleto en ATA ACS-4 Revisión 10 (noviembre de 2015).

Los argumentos válidos para esta opción son:

offline - [ATA] ejecuta la Prueba Fuera de Línea Inmediata SMART. Esto inicia inmediatamente la prueba descrita anteriormente. Este comando se puede dar durante la operación normal del sistema. Los efectos de esta prueba solo son visibles en que actualiza los valores de los Atributos SMART, y si se encuentran errores, aparecerán en el registro de errores SMART, visibles con la opción '-l error'.

Si la opción '-c' a

Auto-pruebas

-t check,N
[ATA only] performs a SMART test on a range of Logical Block Addresses (LBAs), rather than the entire disk. The range is specified by the starting LBA N and the ending LBA M.

For example, the command:

  smartctl -t check,100000000-200000000 /dev/sda
runs a self test on one span consisting of LBAs 100,000,000 to 200,000,000 (inclusive). The command:

  smartctl -t check,100000000-max /dev/sda
runs a self test from LBA 100,000,000 up to the end of the disk. The '-t' option can be given up to five times, to test up to five spans. For example the command:

  smartctl -t check,0-100 -t check,1000-2000 /dev/sda
runs a self test on two spans. The first span consists of 101 LBAs and the second span consists of 1001 LBAs. Note that the spans can overlap partially or completely, for example:

  smartctl -t check,0-10 -t check,5-15 -t check,10-20 /dev/sda
The results of the selective self-test can be obtained (both during and after the test) by printing the SMART self-test log, using the '-l selftest' option to smartctl.

Selective self-tests are particularly useful as disk capacities increase: an extended self test (smartctl -t long) can take several hours. Selective self-tests are helpful if (based on SYSLOG error messages, previous failed self-tests, or SMART error log entries) you suspect that a disk is having problems at a particular range of Logical Block Addresses (LBAs).

Selective self-tests can be run during normal system operation (unless done in captive mode - see the '-C' option below).

The following variants of the selective self-test command use spans based on the ranges from past tests already stored on the disk:

check,redo[+SIZE] - [ATA only] redo the last SMART Selective Self Test using the same LBA range. The starting LBA is identical to the LBA used by last test, same for ending LBA unless a new span size is specified by optional +SIZE argument.

For example the commands:

  smartctl -t check,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,redo /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,redo+20 /dev/sda
have the same effect as: 
  smartctl -t check,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,10-29 /dev/sda

check,next[+SIZE] - [ATA only] runs a SMART Selective Self Test on the LBA range which follows the range of the last test. The starting LBA is set to (ending LBA +1) of the last test. A new span size may be specified by the optional +SIZE argument.

For example the commands:

  smartctl -t check,0-999 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,next /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,next+2000 /dev/sda
have the same effect as: 
  smartctl -t check,0-999 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,1000-1999 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t check,2000-3999 /dev/sda

If the last test ended at the last LBA of the disk, the new range starts at LBA 0. The span size of the last span of a disk is adjusted such that the total number of spans to check the full disk will not be changed by future uses of '-t check,next'.

check,cont[+SIZE] - [ATA only] performs a 'redo' (above) if the self test status reports that the last test was aborted by the host. Otherwise it run the 'next' (above) test.

afterselect,on - [ATA only] perform an offline read scan after a Selective self-test has completed. This option must be used together with one or more of the select,N-M options above. If the LBAs that have been specified in the Selective self-test pass the test with no errors found, then read scan the remainder of the disk. If the device is powered-cycled while this read scan is in progress, the read scan will be automatically resumed after a time specified by the pending timer (see below). The value of this option is preserved between selective self-tests.

afterselect,off - [ATA only] do not read scan the remainder of the disk after a Selective self-test has completed. This option must be use together with one or more of the select,N-M options above. The value of this option is preserved between selective self-tests.

pending,N - [ATA only] set the pending offline read scan timer to N minutes. Here N is an integer in the range from 0 to 65535 inclusive. If the device is powered off during a read scan after a Selective self-test, then resume the test automatically N minutes after power-up. This option must be use together with one or more of the select,N-M options above. The value of this option is preserved between selective self-tests.

vendor,N - [ATA only] issues the ATA command SMART EXECUTE OFF-LINE IMMEDIATE with subcommand N in LBA LOW register. The subcommand is specified as a hex value in the range 0x00 to 0xff. Subcommands 0x40–0x7e and 0x90–0xff are reserved for vendor specific use, see table 61 of T13/1699-D Revision 6a (ATA8-ACS). Note that the subcommands 0x00–0x04, 0x7f, 0x81–0x84 are supported by other smartctl options (e.g. 0x01: '-t short', 0x7f: '-X', 0x82: '-C -t long').

WARNING: Only run subcommands documented by the vendor of the device.

Example for some Intel SSDs only: The subcommand 0x40 ('-t vendor,0x40') clears the timed workload related SMART attributes (226, 227, 228). Note that the raw values of these attributes are held at 65535 (0xffff) until the workload timer reaches 60 minutes.

force - start new self-test even if another test is already running. By default a running self-test will not be interrupted to begin another test.

offline
[SCSI] runs the default self test in foreground. No entry is placed in the self test log.
short
[ATA] runs SMART Short Self Test (usually under ten minutes). This command can be given during normal system operation (unless run in captive mode - see the '-C' option below). This is a test in a different category than the immediate or automatic offline tests. The "Self" tests check the electrical and mechanical performance as well as the read performance of the disk. Their results are reported in the Self Test Error Log, readable with the '-l selftest' option. Note that on some disks the progress of the self-test can be monitored by watching this log during the self-test; with other disks use the '-c' option to monitor progress.
[SCSI] runs the "Background short" self-test.
[NVMe: NEW EXPERIMENTAL SMARTCTL 7.4 FEATURE] runs the "Short" self-test for current namespace.
long
[ATA] runs SMART Extended Self Test (tens of minutes to several hours). This is a longer and more thorough version of the Short Self Test described above. Note that this command can be given during normal system operation (unless run in captive mode - see the '-C' option below).
[SCSI] runs the "Background long" self-test.
[NVMe: NEW EXPERIMENTAL SMARTCTL 7.4 FEATURE] runs the "Extended" self-test for current namespace.
conveyance
[ATA only] runs a SMART Conveyance Self Test (minutes). This self-test routine is intended to identify damage incurred during transporting of the device. This self-test routine should take on the order of minutes to complete. Note that this command can be given during normal system operation (unless run in captive mode - see the '-C' option below).
select,N-M, select,N+SIZE
[ATA only] runs a SMART Selective Self Test, to test a range of disk Logical Block Addresses (LBAs), rather than the entire disk. Each range of LBAs that is checked is called a "span" and is specified by a starting LBA (N) and an ending LBA (M) with N less than or equal to M. The range can also be specified as N+SIZE. A span at the end of a disk can be specified by N-max.
For example the commands:
  smartctl -t select,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,10+11 /dev/sda
both runs a self test on one span consisting of LBAs ten to twenty (inclusive). The command:
  smartctl -t select,100000000-max /dev/sda
run a self test from LBA 100000000 up to the end of the disk. The '-t' option can be given up to five times, to test up to five spans. For example the command:
  smartctl -t select,0-100 -t select,1000-2000 /dev/sda
runs a self test on two spans. The first span consists of 101 LBAs and the second span consists of 1001 LBAs. Note that the spans can overlap partially or completely, for example:
  smartctl -t select,0-10 -t select,5-15 -t select,10-20 /dev/sda
The results of the selective self-test can be obtained (both during and after the test) by printing the SMART self-test log, using the '-l selftest' option to smartctl.
Selective self tests are particularly useful as disk capacities increase: an extended self test (smartctl -t long) can take several hours. Selective self-tests are helpful if (based on SYSLOG error messages, previous failed self-tests, or SMART error log entries) you suspect that a disk is having problems at a particular range of Logical Block Addresses (LBAs).
Selective self-tests can be run during normal system operation (unless done in captive mode - see the '-C' option below).
The following variants of the selective self-test command use spans based on the ranges from past tests already stored on the disk:
select,redo[+SIZE] - [ATA only] redo the last SMART Selective Self Test using the same LBA range. The starting LBA is identical to the LBA used by last test, same for ending LBA unless a new span size is specified by optional +SIZE argument.
For example the commands:
  smartctl -t select,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,redo /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,redo+20 /dev/sda
have the same effect as: 
  smartctl -t select,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,10-20 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,10-29 /dev/sda
select,next[+SIZE] - [ATA only] runs a SMART Selective Self Test on the LBA range which follows the range of the last test. The starting LBA is set to (ending LBA +1) of the last test. A new span size may be specified by the optional +SIZE argument.
For example the commands:
  smartctl -t select,0-999 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,next /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,next+2000 /dev/sda
have the same effect as: 
  smartctl -t select,0-999 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,1000-1999 /dev/sda
  smartctl -t select,2000-3999 /dev/sda
If the last test ended at the last LBA of the disk, the new range starts at LBA 0. The span size of the last span of a disk is adjusted such that the total number of spans to check the full disk will not be changed by future uses of '-t select,next'.
select,cont[+SIZE] - [ATA only] performs a 'redo' (above) if the self test status reports that the last test was aborted by the host. Otherwise it run the 'next' (above) test.
afterselect,on - [ATA only] perform an offline read scan after a Selective self-test has completed. This option must be used together with one or more of the select,N-M options above. If the LBAs that have been specified in the Selective self-test pass the test with no errors found, then read scan the remainder of the disk. If the device is powered-cycled while this read scan is in progress, the read scan will be automatically resumed after a time specified by the pending timer (see below). The value of this option is preserved between selective self-tests.
afterselect,off - [ATA only] do not read scan the remainder of the disk after a Selective self-test has completed. This option must be use together with one or more of the select,N-M options above. The value of this option is preserved between selective self-tests.
pending,N - [ATA only] set the pending offline read scan timer to N minutes. Here N is an integer in the range from 0 to 65535 inclusive. If the device is powered off during a read scan after a Selective self-test, then resume the test automatically N minutes after power-up. This option must be use together with one or more of the select,N-M options above. The value of this option is preserved between selective self-tests.
vendor,N - [ATA only] issues the ATA command SMART EXECUTE OFF-LINE IMMEDIATE with subcommand N in LBA LOW register. The subcommand is specified as a hex value in the range 0x00 to 0xff. Subcommands 0x40–0x7e and 0x90–0xff are reserved for vendor specific use, see table 61 of T13/1699-D Revision 6a (ATA8-ACS). Note that the subcommands 0x00–0x04, 0x7f, 0x81–0x84 are supported by other smartctl options (e.g. 0x01: '-t short', 0x7f: '-X', 0x82: '-C -t long').
WARNING: Only run subcommands documented by the vendor of the device.
Example for some Intel SSDs only: The subcommand 0x40 ('-t vendor,0x40') clears the timed workload related SMART attributes (226, 227, 228). Note that the raw values of these attributes are held at 65535 (0xffff) until the workload timer reaches 60 minutes.
force - start new self-test even if another test is already running. By default a running self-test will not be interrupted to begin another test.

Ajuste de Auto-pruebas

-c, --completion-info
Show completion status of background SMART tests (ATA). This information is updated periodically. If the device is polled for this information frequently enough, it is possible to track the progress of a background test. Note that if the device has the "Suspend Offline collection upon new command" capability, then you can track the progress of the Immediate Offline test using the '-c' option to smartctl. If the '-c' option show that the device has the "Abort Offline collection upon new command" capability then most commands will abort the Immediate Offline Test, so you should not try to track the progress of the test with '-c', as it will abort the test.
-C, --captive
[ATA] Runs self-tests in captive mode. This has no effect with '-t offline' or if the '-t' option is not used.

WARNING: Tests run in captive mode may busy out the drive for the length of the test. Only run captive tests on drives without any mounted partitions!

[SCSI] Runs the self-test in "Foreground" mode.

-X, --abort
Aborts non-captive SMART Self Tests. Note that this command will abort the Offline Immediate Test routine only if your disk has the "Abort Offline collection upon new command" capability.

Conjuntos de comandos ATA, SCSI y SAT

En el pasado, existía una clara distinción entre los dispositivos de almacenamiento que utilizaban los conjuntos de comandos ATA y SCSI. Esta distinción a menudo se reflejaba en su denominación y hardware. Ahora, varios transportes SCSI (por ejemplo, SAS, FC e iSCSI) pueden interconectarse tanto con discos SCSI (por ejemplo, FC y SAS) como con discos ATA (especialmente SATA). Los dispositivos de almacenamiento USB y IEEE 1394 utilizan el conjunto de comandos SCSI externamente, pero casi siempre contienen discos ATA o SATA (o flash). Los subsistemas de almacenamiento en algunos sistemas operativos han comenzado a eliminar la distinción entre ATA y SCSI en sus políticas de denominación de dispositivos.

El 99% de las operaciones que un SO realiza en un disco implican los comandos SCSI INQUIRY, READ CAPACITY, READ y WRITE, o sus equivalentes ATA. Dado que los comandos SCSI son ligeramente más generales que sus equivalentes ATA, muchos SO generan comandos SCSI (principalmente READ y WRITE) y dejan que un nivel inferior los traduzca a sus equivalentes ATA según sea necesario. Una nota importante aquí es que el "nivel inferior" puede estar en equipos externos y, por lo tanto, fuera del control de un SO.

SCSI to ATA Translation (SAT) es un estándar (ANSI INCITS 431-2007) que especifica cómo se realiza esta traducción. Para el 1% restante de las operaciones que un SO realiza en un disco, SAT proporciona dos opciones. La primera es un comando opcional ATA PASS-THROUGH SCSI (hay dos variantes). La segunda es una traducción del comando SCSI más cercano. El interés actual se centra principalmente en la opción "pass-through".

| ATAP-LOG,device,log,ATAP-LOG,device, un buen lugar para empezar es con las Secciones 4.8 y 6.54 del primer volumen de la especificación 'ATA Attachment with Packet Interface-7' (ATA/ATAPI-7) Revisión 4b. Este documento describe la funcionalidad SMART a la que las utilidades smartmontools proporcionan acceso.

El funcionamiento de SMART se definió originalmente en las especificaciones SFF-8035i revisión 2 y SFF-8055i revisión 1.4. Estas son publicaciones del Comité de Factores de Forma Pequeña (SFF).

Los enlaces a estos y otros documentos se pueden encontrar en la página Enlaces del Wiki de smartmontools en https://www.smartmontools.org/wiki/Links.
